```
100→    3. Presort   — stamp plan positions, separate skipped items
   101→    4. Lifecycle — filter endgame-only items when objective work remains
   102→    5. Sort      — rank by impact/confidence, apply plan order
   103→    6. Limit     — truncate to count, optionally add explain metadata
   104→    """
   105→    opts = options or QueueBuildOptions()
   106→    plan, scan_path, status, threshold = _resolve_inputs(opts, state)
   107→
   108→    # 1. Gather
   109→    items = build_issue_items(
   110→        state, scan_path=scan_path, status_filter=status,
   111→        scope=opts.scope, chronic=opts.chronic,
   112→    )
   113→    items += _gather_subjective_items(state, opts, threshold)
   114→    items += _gather_workflow_items(state, plan, status)
   115→
   116→    # 2. Score & filter
   117→    enrich_with_impact(items, state.get("dimension_scores", {}))
   118→    items = [i for i in items if _passes_impact_floor(i)]
   119→
   120→    # 3. Plan-aware ordering (part 1: separate skipped items)
   121→    new_ids, skipped = _plan_presort(items, state, plan)
   122→
   123→    # 4. Lifecycle filter — endgame-only items filtered when objective work remains
   124→    items = _apply_lifecycle_filter(items)
   125→
   126→    # 5. Sort & plan post-processing
   127→    items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   128→    _plan_postsort(items, skipped, plan, opts)
   129→
   130→    # 6. Finalize
   131→    if not items:
   132→        items += _empty_queue_fallback(plan)
   133→    total = len(items)
   134→    if opts.count is not None and opts.count > 0:
   135→        items = items[:opts.count]
   136→    if opts.explain:
   137→        for item in items:
   138→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   139→
   140→    return {
   141→        "items": items,
   142→        "total": total,
   143→        "grouped": group_queue_items(items, "item"),
   144→        "new_ids": new_ids,
   145→    }
   146→
   147→
   148→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   149→# Pipeline helpers (private to this module)
   150→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   151→
   152→
   153→def _resolve_inputs(
   154→    opts: QueueBuildOptions, state: StateModel,
   155→) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None, str, float]:
   156→    """Resolve plan, scan_path, status, and subjective threshold from options."""
   157→    ctx = opts.context
   158→    plan = ctx.plan if ctx is not None else opts.plan
   159→
   160→    scan_path: str | None = (
   161→        state.get("scan_path")
   162→        if opts.scan_path is _SCAN_PATH_FROM_STATE
   163→        else opts.scan_path  # type: ignore[assignment]
   164→    )
   165→
   166→    status = opts.status
   167→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
   168→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
   169→
   170→    try:
   171→        threshold = float(opts.subjective_threshold)
   172→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   173→        threshold = 100.0
   174→    threshold = max(0.0, min(100.0, threshold))
   175→
   176→    return plan, scan_path, status, threshold
   177→
   178→
   179→def _gather_subjective_items(
   180→    state: StateModel,
   181→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   182→    threshold: float,
   183→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   184→    """Build subjective dimension candidates.
   185→
   186→    Lifecycle filtering (endgame gating) happens in _apply_lifecycle_filter,
   187→    not here. This function only handles configuration and scope.
   188→    """
   189→    if not opts.include_subjective:
   190→        return []
   191→    if opts.status not in {"open", "all"}:
   192→        return []
   193→    if opts.chronic:
   194→        return []
   195→
   196→    candidates = build_subjective_items(
   197→        state, state.get("issues", {}), threshold=threshold,
   198→    )
   199→    return [item for item in candidates if scope_matches(item, opts.scope)]
   200→
   201→
   202→def _gather_workflow_items(
   203→    state: StateModel, plan: dict | None, status: str,
   204→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   205→    """Inject triage stages, checkpoints, and create-plan when plan is active."""
   206→    if not plan or status not in {"open", "all"}:
   207→        return []
   208→
   209→    items: list[WorkQueueItem] = list(build_triage_stage_items(plan, state))
   210→    for builder in (
   211→        build_score_checkpoint_item,
   212→        build_import_scores_item,
   213→        build_communicate_score_item,
   214→    ):
   215→        item = builder(plan, state)
   216→        if item is not None:
   217→            items.append(item)
   218→    plan_item = build_create_plan_item(plan)
   219→    if plan_item is not None:
   220→        items.append(plan_item)
   221→    return items
   222→
   223→
   224→_MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT = 0.05
   225→
   226→
   227→def _passes_impact_floor(item: WorkQueueItem) -> bool:
   228→    """Return True if item should survive the impact floor filter."""
   229→    if item.get("kind") != "issue":
   230→        return True
   231→    if item.get("is_review") or item.get("is_subjective"):
   232→        return True
   233→    impact = item.get("estimated_impact")
   234→    return not impact or impact >= _MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT
   235→
   236→
   237→def _plan_presort(
   238→    items: list[WorkQueueItem], state: StateModel, plan: dict | None,
   239→) -> tuple[set[str], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
   240→    """Enrich plan metadata and stamp sort keys before sorting.
   241→
   242→    Returns ``(new_ids, skipped)`` — skipped items are removed from
   243→    ``items`` in place and returned separately for post-sort re-append.
   244→    """
   245→    if not plan:
   246→        return set(), []
   247→
   248→    new_ids = _new_item_ids(state)
   249→    enrich_plan_metadata(items, plan)
   250→    stamp_plan_sort_keys(items, plan, new_ids)
   251→    remaining, skipped = separate_skipped(items, plan)
   252→    items[:] = remaining
   253→    return new_ids, skipped
   254→
   255→
   256→def _plan_postsort(
   257→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
   258→    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem],
   259→    plan: dict | None,
   260→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   261→) -> None:
   262→    """Re-append skipped items, stamp positions, filter to cluster focus."""
   263→    if not plan:
   264→        return
   265→
   266→    if opts.include_skipped:
   267→        items.extend(skipped)
   268→    stamp_positions(items, plan)
   269→    focused = filter_cluster_focus(items, plan, opts.cluster)
   270→    items[:] = focused
   271→
   272→
   273→def _has_objective_items(items: list[WorkQueueItem]) -> bool:
   274→    """True if any objective mechanical work items remain in the queue."""
   275→    return any(
   276→        i.get("kind") == "issue"
   277→        and i.get("detector", "") not in NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   278→        for i in items
   279→    )
   280→
   281→
   282→def _is_endgame_only(item: WorkQueueItem) -> bool:
   283→    """True if this item should only appear when the objective queue is drained."""
   284→    return (
   285→        item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension"
   286→        and not item.get("initial_review")
   287→    )
   288→
   289→
   290→def _apply_lifecycle_filter(items: list[WorkQueueItem]) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   291→    """Enforce lifecycle visibility rules.
   292→
   293→    Endgame-only items (subjective reassessment) are filtered out when
   294→    objective mechanical work remains. Initial review items always survive
   295→    — they're onboarding priority, not endgame work.
   296→    """
   297→    if not _has_objective_items(items):
   298→        return items  # endgame: everything visible
   299→    return [i for i in items if not _is_endgame_only(i)]
   300→
   301→
   302→def _empty_queue_fallback(plan: dict | None) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   303→    """Return a 'run scan' nudge when an active plan cycle has cleared."""
   304→    if not plan:
   305→        return []
   306→    plan_scores = plan.get("plan_start_scores", {})
   307→    if plan_scores.get("strict") is None:
   308→        return []
   309→    return [{
   310→        "id": "workflow::run-scan",
   311→        "kind": "workflow_action",
   312→        "summary": "Queue cleared \u2014 run scan to finalize and reveal your updated score.",
   313→        "primary_command": "desloppify scan",
   314→        "file": "",
   315→        "detector": "workflow",
   316→        "confidence": "high",
   317→    }]
   318→
   319→
   320→__all__ = [
   321→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   322→    "QueueBuildOptions",
   323→    "QueueContext",
   324→    "WorkQueueResult",
   325→    "build_work_queue",
   326→    "collapse_clusters",
   327→    "group_queue_items",
   328→]
```

```
    13→def add_plan_parser(sub) -> None:
    14→    p_plan = sub.add_parser(
    15→        "plan",
    16→        help="Living plan: generate, reorder, cluster, skip, note",
    17→        description="""\
    18→Manage the living plan — a persistent layer on top of the work queue.
    19→Track custom ordering, clusters, skips, and per-issue annotations.
    20→Run with no subcommand to generate a full prioritized markdown plan.""",
    21→        epilog="""\
    22→typical workflow:
    23→  desloppify scan                       # detect issues
    24→  desloppify plan                       # full prioritized markdown
    25→  desloppify plan queue                 # compact table of all items
    26→  desloppify plan cluster create ...    # group related issues
    27→  desloppify plan focus <cluster>       # narrow scope
    28→  desloppify next                       # work on the next item
    29→  desloppify plan resolve <id> --attest .. # mark as fixed
    30→
    31→patterns (used by reorder, skip, resolve, describe, note, etc.):
    32→  Patterns match issues by detector, file, ID prefix, glob, or name.
    33→  Cluster names also work as patterns — they expand to all member IDs.
    34→  Examples: "security", "src/foo.py", "unused::*React*", "my-cluster"
    35→
    36→subcommands:
    37→  show       Show plan metadata summary
    38→  queue      Compact table of upcoming queue items
    39→  reset      Reset plan to empty
    40→  reorder    Reposition issues or clusters in the queue
    41→  resolve    Mark issues as fixed (score movement + next-step)
    42→  describe   Set augmented description
    43→  note       Set note on issues
    44→  skip       Skip issues (temporary/permanent/false_positive)
    45→  unskip     Bring skipped issues back to queue
    46→  reopen     Reopen resolved issues
    47→  focus      Set or clear active cluster focus
    48→  cluster    Manage issue clusters
    49→  triage     Staged triage workflow (after review)""",
    50→        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    51→    )

    53→    p_plan.add_argument(
    54→        "--output", type=str, metavar="FILE", help="Write to file instead of stdout"

    73→    plan_sub.add_parser("reset", help="Reset plan to empty")
    74→
    75→    # plan reorder <patterns> <position> [--target TARGET]
    76→    p_move = plan_sub.add_parser(
    77→        "reorder",
    78→        help="Reposition issues in the queue",
    79→        epilog="""\
    80→patterns accept issue IDs, detector names, file paths, globs, or cluster names.

    82→
    83→examples:
    84→  desloppify plan reorder security top                         # all issues from detector
```

```
1→"""Compact queue table renderer for ``plan queue``."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.guardrails import print_triage_guardrail_info
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import (
     9→    QueueBreakdown,
    10→    format_queue_headline,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
    14→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize, print_table
    15→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.core import (
    16→    QueueBuildOptions,
    17→    build_work_queue,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.plan_order import collapse_clusters
    20→from desloppify.engine.plan import compute_new_issue_ids, load_plan
    21→
    22→
    23→def _truncate(text: str, width: int) -> str:
    24→    if len(text) <= width:
    25→        return text
    26→    return text[: width - 1] + "\u2026"
    27→
    28→
    29→def _print_queue_header(
    30→    *,
    31→    items: list[dict],
    32→    include_skipped: bool,
    33→    plan: dict,
    34→    new_count: int = 0,
    35→) -> None:
    36→    total = len(items)
    37→    skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))
    38→    non_skipped = total - skipped_count
    39→    plan_skipped_total = len(plan.get("skipped", {}))
    40→
    41→    # Count subjective items in the visible list
    42→    subjective = sum(
    43→        1 for it in items if it.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension"
    44→    )
    45→
    46→    # Count plan-ordered items (minus skipped)
    47→    queue_order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    48→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    49→    plan_ordered = sum(1 for fid in queue_order if fid not in skipped_ids)
    50→
    51→    breakdown = QueueBreakdown(
    52→        queue_total=non_skipped,
    53→        plan_ordered=plan_ordered,
    54→        skipped=plan_skipped_total,
    55→        subjective=subjective,
    56→    )
    57→    headline = format_queue_headline(breakdown)
    58→    new_suffix = f"  ({new_count} new this scan)" if new_count > 0 else ""
    59→    print(colorize(f"\n  {headline}{new_suffix}", "bold"))
    60→
    61→    focus = plan.get("active_cluster")
    62→    if focus:
    63→        print(colorize(f"  Focus: {focus}", "cyan"))
    64→
    65→    if include_skipped or skipped_count != 0:
    66→        return
    67→    if not plan_skipped_total:
    68→        return
    69→    print(
    70→        colorize(
    71→            f"  ({plan_skipped_total} skipped item{'s' if plan_skipped_total != 1 else ''}"
    72→            " hidden — use --include-skipped)",
    73→            "dim",
    74→        )
    75→    )
    76→
    77→
    78→def _queue_display_items(items: list[dict], *, top: int) -> list[dict]:
    79→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
    80→        return items[:top]
    81→    return items
    82→
    83→
    84→def _build_rows(display_items: list[dict], new_ids: set[str] | None = None) -> list[list[str]]:
    85→    rows: list[list[str]] = []
    86→    _new = new_ids or set()
    87→    for idx, item in enumerate(display_items, 1):
    88→        pos = str(idx)
    89→        kind = item.get("kind", "issue")
    90→
    91→        if kind == "workflow_stage":
    92→            blocked = item.get("is_blocked", False)
    93→            blocked_tag = " [blocked]" if blocked else ""
    94→            conf_str = "—"
    95→            detector = "planning"
    96→            summary = f"[TRIAGE] {item.get('summary', '')}{blocked_tag}"
    97→            cluster_name = ""
    98→        elif kind == "workflow_action":
    99→            conf_str = "—"
   100→            detector = "workflow"
   101→            summary = item.get("summary", "")
   102→            cluster_name = ""
   103→        elif kind == "cluster":
   104→            conf_str = item.get("confidence", "high")
   105→            member_count = item.get("member_count", 0)
   106→            detector = item.get("detector", "cluster")
   107→            new_in_cluster = sum(
   108→                1 for m in item.get("members", [])
   109→                if m.get("id") in _new
   110→            )
   111→            new_tag = f" (+{new_in_cluster} new)" if new_in_cluster else ""
   112→            summary = f"[{member_count} items{new_tag}] {item.get('summary', '')}"
   113→            cluster_name = item.get("cluster_name", item.get("id", ""))
   114→        else:
   115→            conf_str = item.get("confidence", "medium")
   116→            detector = item.get("detector", "")
   117→            summary = item.get("summary", "")
   118→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   119→            cluster_name = plan_cluster.get("name", "") if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else ""
   120→
   121→        prefix = "* " if item.get("id") in _new else ""
   122→        suffix = " [skip]" if item.get("plan_skipped") else ""
   123→        summary_display = _truncate(prefix + summary, 48) + suffix
   124→        rows.append([pos, conf_str, detector, summary_display, cluster_name])
   125→    return rows
   126→
   127→
   128→def cmd_plan_queue(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   129→    """Render a compact table of all upcoming queue items."""
   130→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   131→    state = runtime.state
   132→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   133→        return
   134→
   135→    top = getattr(args, "top", 30)
   136→    cluster_filter = getattr(args, "cluster", None)
   137→    include_skipped = bool(getattr(args, "include_skipped", False))
   138→
   139→    plan = load_plan()
   140→    print_triage_guardrail_info(plan=plan, state=state)
   141→
   142→    effective_cluster = cluster_filter
   143→    if not cluster_filter:
   144→        active_cluster = plan.get("active_cluster")
   145→        if active_cluster:
   146→            effective_cluster = active_cluster
   147→
   148→    queue = build_work_queue(
   149→        state,
   150→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   151→            count=None,
   152→            status="open",
   153→            include_subjective=True,
   154→            plan=plan,
   155→            include_skipped=include_skipped,
   156→            cluster=effective_cluster,
   157→        ),
   158→    )
   159→    items = queue.get("items", [])
   160→    # Collapse auto-clusters into display meta-items
   161→    if plan and not effective_cluster and not plan.get("active_cluster"):
   162→        items = collapse_clusters(items, plan)
   163→
   164→    sort_by = getattr(args, "sort", "priority")
   165→    all_new_ids: set[str] = queue.get("new_ids", set())
   166→    # Merge review-based new issue IDs (since last triage)
   167→    review_new_ids = compute_new_issue_ids(plan, state)
   168→    all_new_ids = all_new_ids | review_new_ids
   169→    item_ids = {it.get("id") for it in items}
   170→    new_ids = all_new_ids & item_ids
   171→
   172→    if sort_by == "recent":
   173→        items = sorted(items, key=lambda it: it.get("first_seen", ""), reverse=True)
   174→
   175→    _print_queue_header(
   176→        items=items,
   177→        include_skipped=include_skipped,
   178→        plan=plan,
   179→        new_count=len(new_ids),
   180→    )
   181→
   182→    if not items:
   183→        print(colorize("\n  Queue is empty.", "green"))
   184→        return
   185→
   186→    # Determine which items to show
   187→    display_items = _queue_display_items(items, top=top)
   188→    headers = ["#", "Confidence", "Detector", "Summary", "Cluster"]
   189→    rows = _build_rows(display_items, new_ids=new_ids)
   190→
   191→    print()
   192→    widths = [4, 4, 12, 50, 16]
   193→    print_table(headers, rows, widths=widths)
   194→
   195→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
   196→        remaining = len(items) - top
   197→        print(colorize(
   198→            f"\n  ... and {remaining} more (use --top 0 to show all)", "dim"
   199→        ))
   200→    print()
   201→
   202→
   203→__all__ = ["cmd_plan_queue"]
   204→
```

```
1→"""Helpers for tracking user-promoted queue IDs."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any
     6→
     7→
     8→def _promoted_ids(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
     9→    promoted = plan.get("promoted_ids")
    10→    if isinstance(promoted, list):
    11→        return promoted
    12→    promoted = []
    13→    plan["promoted_ids"] = promoted
    14→    return promoted
    15→
    16→
    17→def add_promoted_ids(plan: dict[str, Any], issue_ids: list[str]) -> None:
    18→    """Append issue IDs to promoted_ids preserving existing order."""
    19→    promoted = _promoted_ids(plan)
    20→    existing = set(promoted)
    21→    for issue_id in issue_ids:
    22→        if issue_id in existing:
    23→            continue
    24→        promoted.append(issue_id)
    25→        existing.add(issue_id)
    26→
    27→
    28→def prune_promoted_ids(plan: dict[str, Any], issue_ids: set[str] | list[str]) -> None:
    29→    """Remove issue IDs from promoted_ids if present."""
    30→    remove_set = set(issue_ids)
    31→    if not remove_set:
    32→        return
    33→    promoted = _promoted_ids(plan)
    34→    plan["promoted_ids"] = [issue_id for issue_id in promoted if issue_id not in remove_set]
    35→
    36→
    37→def promoted_insertion_index(order: list[str], plan: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
    38→    """Return insertion index immediately after the last promoted item."""
    39→    promoted = set(_promoted_ids(plan))
    40→    if not promoted:
    41→        return 0
    42→    last_idx = -1
    43→    for idx, issue_id in enumerate(order):
    44→        if issue_id in promoted:
    45→            last_idx = idx
    46→    return last_idx + 1 if last_idx >= 0 else 0
    47→
    48→
    49→__all__ = [
    50→    "add_promoted_ids",
    51→    "promoted_insertion_index",
    52→    "prune_promoted_ids",
    53→]
    54→
```

```
1→"""State resolution operations (match + resolve)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "coerce_assessment_score",
     9→    "match_issues",
    10→    "resolve_issues",
    11→]
    12→
    13→from desloppify.base.text_utils import is_numeric
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import _matches_pattern
    15→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    16→    StateModel,
    17→    ensure_state_defaults,
    18→    utc_now,
    19→    validate_state_invariants,
    20→)
    21→
    22→
    23→from desloppify.engine._state import _recompute_stats
    24→
    25→
    26→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    27→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    28→
    29→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    30→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    31→    """
    32→    if is_numeric(value):
    33→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    34→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    35→        raw = value.get("score")
    36→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    37→            return None
    38→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    39→    return None
    40→
    41→
    42→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    43→    state: StateModel,
    44→    *,
    45→    status: str,
    46→    resolved_issues: list[dict],
    47→    now: str,
    48→) -> None:
    49→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review issues are resolved.
    50→
    51→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    52→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    53→    for a re-review.
    54→    """
    55→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    56→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    57→        return
    58→
    59→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    60→    for issue in resolved_issues:
    61→        if issue.get("detector") != "review":
    62→            continue
    63→        dimension = str(issue.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    64→        if dimension:
    65→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    66→
    67→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    68→        if dimension not in assessments:
    69→            continue
    70→
    71→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    72→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    73→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    74→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_issue_{status}"
    75→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    76→        else:
    77→            assessments[dimension] = {
    78→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    79→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    80→                "refresh_reason": f"review_issue_{status}",
    81→                "stale_since": now,
    82→            }
    83→
    84→
    85→def match_issues(
    86→    state: StateModel, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open"
    87→) -> list[dict]:
    88→    """Return issues matching *pattern* with the given status."""
    89→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    90→    return [
    91→        issue
    92→        for issue_id, issue in state["issues"].items()
    93→        if not issue.get("suppressed")
    94→        if (status_filter == "all" or issue["status"] == status_filter)
    95→        and _matches_pattern(issue_id, issue, pattern)
    96→    ]
    97→
    98→
    99→def resolve_issues(
   100→    state: StateModel,
   101→    pattern: str,
   102→    status: str,
   103→    note: str | None = None,
   104→    attestation: str | None = None,
   105→) -> list[str]:
   106→    """Set issue status for matches and return affected issue IDs."""
   107→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   108→    now = utc_now()
   109→    resolved: list[str] = []
   110→    resolved_issues: list[dict] = []
   111→    status_filter = "all" if status == "open" else "open"
   112→    for issue in match_issues(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter):
   113→        previous_status = str(issue.get("status", "open")).strip() or "open"
   114→        if status == "open" and previous_status == "open":
   115→            continue
   116→
   117→        extra_updates: dict[str, object] = {}
   118→        if status == "wontfix":
   119→            snapshot_scan_count = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   120→            extra_updates["wontfix_scan_count"] = snapshot_scan_count
   121→            extra_updates["wontfix_snapshot"] = {
   122→                "captured_at": now,
   123→                "scan_count": snapshot_scan_count,
   124→                "tier": issue.get("tier"),
   125→                "confidence": issue.get("confidence"),
   126→                "detail": copy.deepcopy(issue.get("detail", {})),
   127→            }
   128→        if status == "open":
   129→            issue["reopen_count"] = int(issue.get("reopen_count", 0) or 0) + 1
   130→            issue.pop("wontfix_scan_count", None)
   131→            issue.pop("wontfix_snapshot", None)
   132→            previous_note = issue.get("note")
   133→            next_note = note if note is not None else previous_note
   134→            extra_updates["resolved_at"] = None
   135→            extra_updates["note"] = next_note
   136→            reopen_attestation = {
   137→                "kind": "manual_reopen",
   138→                "text": attestation or note,
   139→                "attested_at": now,
   140→                "scan_verified": False,
   141→            }
   142→            reopen_attestation["previous_status"] = previous_status
   143→            extra_updates["resolution_attestation"] = reopen_attestation
   144→
   145→        updates: dict[str, object] = {
   146→            "status": status,
   147→            "note": note,
   148→            "resolved_at": now,
   149→            "suppressed": False,
   150→            "suppressed_at": None,
   151→            "suppression_pattern": None,
   152→            "resolution_attestation": {
   153→                "kind": "manual",
   154→                "text": attestation,
   155→                "attested_at": now,
   156→                "scan_verified": False,
   157→            },
   158→        }
   159→        updates.update(extra_updates)
   160→        issue.update(updates)
   161→        resolved.append(issue["id"])
   162→        resolved_issues.append(issue)
   163→
   164→    _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
   165→        state,
   166→        status=status,
   167→        resolved_issues=resolved_issues,
   168→        now=now,
   169→    )
   170→
   171→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   172→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   173→    return resolved
   174→
```

```
    26→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    27→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    28→
    29→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    30→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    31→    """
    32→    if is_numeric(value):
    33→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    34→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    35→        raw = value.get("score")
    36→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    37→            return None
    38→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    39→    return None
    40→
    41→
    42→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    43→    state: StateModel,
    44→    *,
    45→    status: str,
    46→    resolved_issues: list[dict],
    47→    now: str,
    48→) -> None:
    49→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review issues are resolved.
    50→
    51→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    52→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    53→    for a re-review.
    54→    """
    55→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    56→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    57→        return
    58→
    59→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    60→    for issue in resolved_issues:
    61→        if issue.get("detector") != "review":
    62→            continue
    63→        dimension = str(issue.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    64→        if dimension:
    65→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    66→
    67→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    68→        if dimension not in assessments:
    69→            continue
    70→
    71→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    72→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    73→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    74→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_issue_{status}"
    75→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    76→        else:
    77→            assessments[dimension] = {
    78→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    79→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    80→                "refresh_reason": f"review_issue_{status}",
    81→                "stale_since": now,
    82→            }
    83→

    87→) -> list[dict]:
    88→    """Return issues matching *pattern* with the given status."""

    93→        if not issue.get("suppressed")
    94→        if (status_filter == "all" or issue["status"] == status_filter)
    95→        and _matches_pattern(issue_id, issue, pattern)

    97→
```

```
1→"""State filtering, ignore rules, and issue pattern matching."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→import re
     7→
     8→__all__ = [
     9→    "issue_in_scan_scope",
    10→    "open_scope_breakdown",
    11→    "path_scoped_issues",
    12→    "is_ignored",
    13→    "matched_ignore_pattern",
    14→    "remove_ignored_issues",
    15→    "add_ignore",
    16→    "make_issue",
    17→]
    18→
    19→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    20→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    21→    Issue,
    22→    StateModel,
    23→    ensure_state_defaults,
    24→    utc_now,
    25→    validate_state_invariants,
    26→)
    27→
    28→
    29→def path_scoped_issues(
    30→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→) -> dict[str, Issue]:
    33→    """Filter issues to those within the given scan path."""
    34→    return {
    35→        issue_id: issue
    36→        for issue_id, issue in issues.items()
    37→        if issue_in_scan_scope(str(issue.get("file", "")), scan_path)
    38→    }
    39→
    40→
    41→def issue_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    42→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    43→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    44→        return True
    45→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    46→    return (
    47→        file_path.startswith(prefix)
    48→        or file_path == scan_path
    49→        or file_path == "."
    50→    )
    51→
    52→
    53→def open_scope_breakdown(
    54→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    55→    scan_path: str | None,
    56→    *,
    57→    detector: str | None = None,
    58→) -> dict[str, int]:
    59→    """Return open-issue counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
    60→    in_scope = 0
    61→    out_of_scope = 0
    62→
    63→    for issue in issues.values():
    64→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
    65→            continue
    66→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
    67→            continue
    68→        if detector is not None and issue.get("detector") != detector:
    69→            continue
    70→        file_path = str(issue.get("file", ""))
    71→        if issue_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
    72→            in_scope += 1
    73→        else:
    74→            out_of_scope += 1
    75→
    76→    return {
    77→        "in_scope": in_scope,
    78→        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
    79→        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
    80→    }
    81→
    82→
    83→def is_ignored(issue_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
    84→    """Check if a issue matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
    85→    return matched_ignore_pattern(issue_id, file, ignore_patterns) is not None
    86→
    87→
    88→def matched_ignore_pattern(
    89→    issue_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]
    90→) -> str | None:
    91→    """Return the ignore pattern that matched, if any."""
    92→    for pattern in ignore_patterns:
    93→        if "*" in pattern:
    94→            target = issue_id if "::" in pattern else file
    95→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pattern):
    96→                return pattern
    97→            continue
    98→
    99→        if "::" in pattern:
   100→            if issue_id.startswith(pattern):
   101→                return pattern
   102→            continue
   103→
   104→        raw_base = pattern.rstrip("/")
   105→        rel_base = rel(pattern).rstrip("/")
   106→        for base in (raw_base, rel_base):
   107→            if not base:
   108→                continue
   109→            if file == base or file.startswith(base + "/"):
   110→                return pattern
   111→
   112→    return None
   113→
   114→
   115→def remove_ignored_issues(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   116→    """Suppress issues matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   117→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   118→    matched_ids = [
   119→        issue_id
   120→        for issue_id, issue in state["issues"].items()
   121→        if is_ignored(issue_id, issue["file"], [pattern])
   122→    ]
   123→    now = utc_now()
   124→    for issue_id in matched_ids:
   125→        issue = state["issues"][issue_id]
   126→        issue["suppressed"] = True
   127→        issue["suppressed_at"] = now
   128→        issue["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   129→    from desloppify.engine._scoring.state_integration import (
   130→        recompute_stats as _recompute_stats,
   131→    )
   132→
   133→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   134→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   135→    return len(matched_ids)
   136→
   137→
   138→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   139→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching issues."""
   140→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   141→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   142→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   143→    if pattern not in ignores:
   144→        ignores.append(pattern)
   145→    return remove_ignored_issues(state, pattern)
   146→
   147→
   148→def make_issue(
   149→    detector: str,
   150→    file: str,
   151→    name: str,
   152→    *,
   153→    tier: int,
   154→    confidence: str,
   155→    summary: str,
   156→    detail: dict | None = None,
   157→) -> Issue:
   158→    """Create a normalized issue dict with a stable ID."""
   159→    rfile = rel(file)
   160→    issue_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   161→    now = utc_now()
   162→    return {
   163→        "id": issue_id,
   164→        "detector": detector,
   165→        "file": rfile,
   166→        "tier": tier,
   167→        "confidence": confidence,
   168→        "summary": summary,
   169→        "detail": detail or {},
   170→        "status": "open",
   171→        "note": None,
   172→        "first_seen": now,
   173→        "last_seen": now,
   174→        "resolved_at": None,
   175→        "reopen_count": 0,
   176→    }
   177→
   178→
   179→_HEX8_RE = re.compile(r'^[0-9a-f]{8}$')
   180→
   181→
   182→def _matches_pattern(issue_id: str, issue: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
   183→    """Check if a issue matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, or path."""
   184→    if issue_id == pattern:
   185→        return True
   186→    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(issue_id, pattern):
   187→        return True
   188→    if "::" in pattern and issue_id.startswith(pattern):
   189→        return True
   190→    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and issue_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
   191→        return True
   192→    if (
   193→        issue.get("detector") == pattern
   194→        or issue["file"] == pattern
   195→        or issue["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   196→    ):
   197→        return True
   198→
   199→    # Name-segment fallback: bare name matches the last ::segment of the ID
   200→    if "::" not in pattern and "::" in issue_id:
   201→        segments = issue_id.split("::")
   202→        name_segment = segments[-1]
   203→        if name_segment == pattern:
   204→            return True
   205→        # For hashed IDs, also match the descriptive name (second-to-last segment)
   206→        if len(segments) >= 3 and _HEX8_RE.match(name_segment) and segments[-2] == pattern:
   207→            return True
   208→
   209→    return False
   210→
```

```
    32→def enrich_plan_metadata(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> None:

    45→        if override.get("cluster"):

    61→    """Stamp ``_plan_position`` and ``_is_new`` on each item.
    62→
    63→    These fields are consumed by :func:`item_sort_key` in ``ranking.py``
    64→    to produce the correct final ordering in a single sort pass.
    65→    """
    66→    queue_order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    67→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    68→
    69→    position_map: dict[str, int] = {}
    70→    for idx, issue_id in enumerate(queue_order):
    71→        if issue_id not in skipped_ids:
    72→            position_map[issue_id] = idx
    73→
    74→    for item in items:
    75→        item_id = item["id"]
    76→        pos = position_map.get(item_id)
    77→        item["_plan_position"] = pos  # None if not in queue_order
    78→        item["_is_new"] = item_id in new_ids

    81→def separate_skipped(
    82→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
    83→    plan: dict,
    84→) -> tuple[list[WorkQueueItem], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
    85→    """Separate skipped items from the main list.
    86→
    87→    Returns ``(non_skipped, skipped)`` so callers can optionally re-append.
    88→    """
    89→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    90→    if not skipped_ids:
    91→        return items, []
    92→    non_skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    93→    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    94→    for item in items:
    95→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
    96→            skipped.append(item)
    97→        else:
    98→            non_skipped.append(item)
    99→    return non_skipped, skipped
```

```
   144→def _build_cluster_meta(
   145→    cluster_name: str, members: list[WorkQueueItem], cluster_data: dict[str, Any]
   146→) -> WorkQueueItem:
   147→    """Build a cluster meta-item from its member items."""
   148→    detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
   149→    action = cluster_data.get("action") or ""
   150→    if "desloppify autofix" in action:
   151→        action_type = "auto_fix"
   152→    elif "desloppify move" in action:
   153→        action_type = "reorganize"
   154→    else:
   155→        action_type = action_type_for_detector(detector)
   156→        if action_type == "auto_fix" and "desloppify autofix" not in action:
   157→            action_type = "refactor"
   158→
   159→    stored_desc = cluster_data.get("description") or ""
   160→    total_in_cluster = len(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", []))
   161→    if stored_desc and total_in_cluster != len(members):
   162→        summary = stored_desc.replace(str(total_in_cluster), str(len(members)))
   163→    else:
   164→        summary = stored_desc or f"{len(members)} issues"
   165→
   166→    primary_command = cluster_data.get("action")
   167→    if not primary_command:
   168→        primary_command = f"desloppify next --cluster {cluster_name} --count 10"
   169→
   170→    estimated_impact = max(
   171→        (m.get("estimated_impact", 0.0) for m in members), default=0.0
   172→    )
   173→
   174→    return {
   175→        "id": cluster_name,
   176→        "kind": "cluster",
   177→        "action_type": action_type,
   178→        "summary": summary,
   179→        "members": members,
   180→        "member_count": len(members),
   181→        "primary_command": primary_command,
   182→        "cluster_name": cluster_name,
   183→        "cluster_auto": True,
   184→        "cluster_optional": bool(cluster_data.get("optional")),
   185→        "confidence": "high",
   186→        "detector": detector,
   187→        "file": "",
   188→        "estimated_impact": estimated_impact,
   189→    }
   190→
   191→
   192→def collapse_clusters(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   193→    """Replace cluster member items with single cluster meta-items.
   194→
   195→    Walks the list in order: the first member of each collapsed cluster is
   196→    replaced with its meta-item, subsequent members are skipped.  This
   197→    preserves the ordering established by sort + plan-order.
   198→    """
   199→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   200→    if not clusters:
   201→        return items
   202→
   203→    fid_to_cluster: dict[str, str] = {}
   204→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   205→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   206→            continue
   207→        for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
   208→            fid_to_cluster[issue_id] = name
   209→
   210→    if not fid_to_cluster:
   211→        return items
   212→
   213→    # Collect members per cluster (preserving encounter order)
   214→    cluster_members: dict[str, list[WorkQueueItem]] = {}
   215→    for item in items:
   216→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   217→        if cname:
   218→            cluster_members.setdefault(cname, []).append(item)
   219→
   220→    # Build meta-items only for clusters with 2+ members in the queue
   221→    meta_items: dict[str, WorkQueueItem] = {}
   222→    for cname, members in cluster_members.items():
   223→        if len(members) < 2:
   224→            continue
   225→        meta_items[cname] = _build_cluster_meta(
   226→            cname, members, clusters.get(cname, {})
   227→        )
   228→
   229→    # Walk in order: replace first member of each collapsed cluster
   230→    # with its meta-item, skip subsequent members
   231→    seen_clusters: set[str] = set()
   232→    result: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
   233→    for item in items:
   234→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   235→        if cname and cname in meta_items:
   236→            if cname not in seen_clusters:
   237→                seen_clusters.add(cname)
   238→                result.append(meta_items[cname])
   239→            # skip individual member
   240→        else:
   241→            result.append(item)
   242→    return result
   243→
   244→
   245→__all__ = [
   246→    "collapse_clusters",
   247→    "enrich_plan_metadata",
   248→    "filter_cluster_focus",
   249→    "new_item_ids",
   250→    "separate_skipped",
   251→    "stamp_plan_sort_keys",
   252→    "stamp_positions",
   253→]
   254→
```

## Summary of Findings

I've completed a thorough exploration of the desloppify codebase. Here are the detailed findings organized by your questions:

### 1. QUEUE_ORDER DATA STRUCTURE

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema.py` (lines 160-184)

```python
class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    version: Required[int]
    created: Required[str]
    updated: Required[str]
    queue_order: list[str]  # Line 164: simple list of issue IDs
    deferred: list[str]     # kept empty for migration compat
    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry]
    active_cluster: str | None
    overrides: dict[str, ItemOverride]
    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]
    ...
```

**Key Structure Details:**
- `queue_order` is a simple `list[str]` — just issue IDs in order (line 164)
- It's initialized as empty `[]` in `empty_plan()` (line 193)
- **Constraint:** No ID can appear in both `queue_order` and `skipped` (lines 246-252)
- It defines the primary queue order, independent of cluster membership
- Clusters have their own separate `issue_ids: list[str]` field (line 60)

### 2. HOW CLUSTER MEMBERSHIP RELATES TO QUEUE_ORDER

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/operations_cluster.py` (lines 44-67)

When an item is added to a cluster via `add_to_cluster()`:
```python
def add_to_cluster(plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, issue_ids: list[str]) -> int:
    """Add issue IDs to a cluster. Returns count added."""
    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["issue_ids"]
    for fid in issue_ids:
        if fid not in member_ids:
            member_ids.append(fid)
            count += 1
        # Also updates overrides with cluster assignment
        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
```

**Key Relationship:**
- **Cluster membership and queue_order are INDEPENDENT**
- Adding to a cluster does NOT automatically add to queue_order
- Cluster items can be IN or OUT of queue_order (they're separate concerns)
- The `overrides[issue_id]["cluster"]` field tracks which cluster an item belongs to
- **queue_order is a superset, subset, or disjoint** — no fixed relationship

**From `_resolve.py` (lines 16-24), `_collect_plan_ids()` shows the complete picture:**
```python
def _collect_plan_ids(plan: PlanModel | None) -> set[str]:
    plan_ids: set[str] = set()
    plan_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))        # line 20
    plan_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())      # line 21
    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
        plan_ids.update(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))    # line 23
    return plan_ids
```

These three collections are independent — an ID can exist in:
- queue_order only
- skipped only
- clusters only
- queue_order + clusters (most common working state)

### 3. EXISTING REORDER COMMAND

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/reorder_handlers.py` (lines 14-73)

**Valid reorder operations (from CLI):**
- `top` — move to position 0 (line 32)
- `bottom` — move to end of list (line 34)
- `before <target>` — insert before target ID (line 37)
- `after <target>` — insert after target ID (line 37)
- `up <offset>` — move up by N positions (line 40)
- `down <offset>` — move down by N positions (line 40)

**`resolve_target()` function (lines 14-29):**
```python
def resolve_target(plan: dict, target: str | None, position: str) -> str | None:
    """Resolve a cluster name used as a before/after target to a member ID."""
    if target is None:
        return None
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    if target not in clusters:
        return target  # Already an issue ID
    member_ids = clusters[target].get("issue_ids", [])
    if not member_ids:
        return target
    order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    member_set = set(member_ids)
    ordered = [fid for fid in order if fid in member_set]
    # For "before": use first member, for "after": use last member
    return ordered[0] if position == "before" else ordered[-1]
```

This cleverly resolves cluster names to their boundary members for before/after operations.

### 4. PLAN QUEUE RENDERING

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py` (lines 128-201)

**Flow:**
1. Load plan and build work queue (lines 139-158)
2. **Collapse clusters** (line 162): Replace member items with meta-items
3. Build and render table (lines 186-193)

**How clusters appear in the queue:**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py` (lines 192-242)

```python
def collapse_clusters(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
    """Replace cluster member items with single cluster meta-items.
    
    Walks the list in order: the first member of each collapsed cluster is
    replaced with its meta-item, subsequent members are skipped.
    """
    # Build fid_to_cluster map (line 203-208)
    fid_to_cluster: dict[str, str] = {}
    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
        if not cluster.get("auto"):
            continue
        for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
            fid_to_cluster[issue_id] = name
    
    # Walk in order: replace first member with meta-item (lines 229-241)
    seen_clusters: set[str] = set()
    result: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    for item in items:
        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
        if cname and cname in meta_items:
            if cname not in seen_clusters:
                seen_clusters.add(cname)
                result.append(meta_items[cname])  # Replace first with meta-item
            # Skip subsequent members
        else:
            result.append(item)
    return result
```

**Key insight:** 
- Only **auto-generated clusters** (where `cluster.auto == True`) are collapsed (line 205)
- Manual clusters are NOT collapsed — their members appear individually
- Cluster ordering follows **queue_order** — cluster appears at position of first member
- **Cluster metadata is stamped** via `enrich_plan_metadata()` (line 249 in plan_order.py):
  - `plan_cluster` dict added to each item (lines 45-53)
  - Contains: `name`, `description`, `total_items`, `action_steps`

**Rendering (queue_render.py, lines 84-125):**
```python
def _build_rows(display_items: list[dict], new_ids: set[str] | None = None) -> list[list[str]]:
    for idx, item in enumerate(display_items, 1):
        kind = item.get("kind", "issue")
        
        if kind == "cluster":
            # Cluster meta-item (lines 103-113)
            member_count = item.get("member_count", 0)
            detector = item.get("detector", "cluster")
            new_tag = f" (+{new_in_cluster} new)" if new_in_cluster else ""
            summary = f"[{member_count} items{new_tag}] {item.get('summary', '')}"
            cluster_name = item.get("cluster_name", item.get("id", ""))
        else:
            # Regular issue (lines 114-119)
            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
            cluster_name = plan_cluster.get("name", "") if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else ""
```

The `cluster_name` column shows which cluster each item belongs to.

### 5. THE `_resolve.py` PATTERN MATCHING

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/_resolve.py` (lines 27-58)

```python
def resolve_ids_from_patterns(
    state: StateModel,
    patterns: list[str],
    *,
    plan: PlanModel | None = None,
    status_filter: str = "open",
) -> list[str]:
    """Resolve one or more patterns to a deduplicated list of issue IDs.
    
    When *plan* is provided, literal IDs that exist only in the plan
    (e.g. ``subjective::*`` synthetic items) are included even if they
    have no corresponding entry in ``state["issues"]``.
    """
    seen: set[str] = set()
    result: list[str] = []
    plan_ids = _collect_plan_ids(plan)  # Gathers all plan-resident IDs

    for pattern in patterns:
        # 1. Try state-based pattern matching (line 45)
        matches = match_issues(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter)
        if matches:
            for issue in matches:
                _append_unique(issue["id"], seen, result)
            continue
        
        # 2. Try literal plan ID (line 50)
        if pattern in plan_ids:
            _append_unique(pattern, seen, result)
            continue
        
        # 3. Try cluster name expansion (line 54)
        if plan is not None and pattern in plan.get("clusters", {}):
            for fid in plan["clusters"][pattern].get("issue_ids", []):
                _append_unique(fid, seen, result)
    return result
```

**Pattern matching hierarchy (ordered):**
1. **State pattern matching** (most specific) — via `match_issues()`
2. **Literal plan IDs** (e.g., synthetic items like `subjective::*`)
3. **Cluster name expansion** (matches entire cluster → all member IDs)

**What `match_issues()` supports:**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` (lines 182-209)

```python
def _matches_pattern(issue_id: str, issue: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
    """Check if a issue matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, or path."""
    # 1. Exact ID match (line 184)
    if issue_id == pattern:
        return True
    
    # 2. Glob pattern (line 186)
    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(issue_id, pattern):
        return True
    
    # 3. ID prefix match (e.g., "detector::file") (line 188)
    if "::" in pattern and issue_id.startswith(pattern):
        return True
    
    # 4. Suffix match: 8-char hex hash (line 190)
    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and issue_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
        return True
    
    # 5. Detector or file path match (lines 192-197)
    if (issue.get("detector") == pattern or
        issue["file"] == pattern or
        issue["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")):
        return True
    
    # 6. Name-segment fallback (lines 200-207)
    # Bare name matches the last ::segment of ID
    if "::" not in pattern and "::" in issue_id:
        segments = issue_id.split("::")
        name_segment = segments[-1]
        if name_segment == pattern:
            return True
        # For hashed IDs, also match descriptive name (second-to-last)
        if len(segments) >= 3 and _HEX8_RE.match(name_segment) and segments[-2] == pattern:
            return True
    
    return False
```

**Supported pattern types:**
- Exact ID: `detector::file::name`
- Glob: `detector::*`, `*::name`
- Prefix: `detector::file` (matches all names in that file)
- 8-char hex suffix: `12345678` (matches issues ending in that hash)
- Detector name: `flake8`
- File path: `src/app.py`
- Name segment: `unused_var` (matches last segment)
- Descriptive name in hashed ID: `function_name` (second-to-last segment before 8-char hash)

**When no match is found:**
- Falls back to literal plan ID check (for synthetic items)
- Falls back to cluster name check (expands to all members)
- Returns empty list if nothing matches

**Data access when no matches:**
- `state` dict: all issues, scan metadata (always available)
- `plan` dict: queue_order, clusters, skipped, overrides (passed via `plan=` param)
- The function has full access to both state and plan to make decisions

### 6. QUEUE ORDERING MECHANICS

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/operations_queue.py` (lines 94-135)

```python
def move_items(
    plan: PlanModel,
    issue_ids: list[str],
    position: str,
    target: str | None = None,
    offset: int | None = None,
) -> int:
    """Move issue IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
    # Filter triage IDs (workflow-managed, immutable) (line 107)
    issue_ids = [fid for fid in issue_ids if fid not in TRIAGE_IDS]
    
    # Remove from skipped if present (lines 115-116)
    for fid in issue_ids:
        skipped.pop(fid, None)
    
    # Remove from current position (lines 119-121)
    for fid in issue_ids:
        if fid in order:
            order.remove(fid)
    
    # Resolve insertion point (line 124)
    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, issue_ids)
    
    # Insert in original order (lines 127-128)
    for i, fid in enumerate(issue_ids):
        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
    
    # Track user promotions (only for "top") (lines 131-133)
    if position == "top":
        add_promoted_ids(plan, issue_ids)
    
    return len(issue_ids)
```

**Promoted IDs tracking:**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/promoted_ids.py` (lines 37-46)

```python
def promoted_insertion_index(order: list[str], plan: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
    """Return insertion index immediately after the last promoted item."""
    promoted = set(_promoted_ids(plan))
    if not promoted:
        return 0
    last_idx = -1
    for idx, issue_id in enumerate(order):
        if issue_id in promoted:
            last_idx = idx
    return last_idx + 1 if last_idx >= 0 else 0
```

Items moved to `top` are tracked in `promoted_ids` list, creating a "barrier" — newly discovered items won't push promoted items back down.

### 7. CLUSTER RENDERING WITH OVERRIDE

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py` (lines 32-53)

```python
def enrich_plan_metadata(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> None:
    """Stamp plan description, note, and cluster info from overrides."""
    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
    
    for item in items:
        issue_id = item["id"]
        description = get_issue_description(plan, issue_id)
        note = get_issue_note(plan, issue_id)
        override = get_issue_override(plan, issue_id)
        if override.get("cluster"):
            cluster_name = override["cluster"]
            cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
            item["plan_cluster"] = {
                "name": cluster_name,
                "description": cluster_data.get("description"),
                "total_items": len(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", [])),
                "action_steps": cluster_data.get("action_steps") or [],
            }
```

The `plan_cluster` metadata comes from `overrides[issue_id]["cluster"]` which is set when adding items to clusters (see line 63 in operations_cluster.py).

---

## Architecture Summary

The desloppify queue rendering respects **both** queue_order and cluster membership:

1. **queue_order** controls the primary ordering in the work queue
2. **cluster membership** groups related issues and is tracked separately in `clusters[name].issue_ids`
3. **Plan overrides** track which cluster each issue belongs to via `overrides[issue_id]["cluster"]`
4. **Auto-cluster collapse** replaces cluster members with meta-items for compact display (first member position)
5. **Manual clusters** don't collapse — members display individually with cluster metadata
6. **Pattern resolution** supports full state matching + cluster name expansion + literal plan IDs
7. **Move operations** work on queue_order directly and can target cluster boundary members

   ```python
   3. Call resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   4. If empty result:
   ```

   - **Signature:** `def resolve_ids_from_patterns(state: StateModel, patterns: list[str], *, plan: PlanModel | None = None, status_filter: str = "open") -> list[str]:`
   
   **Resolution Logic:**
   For each pattern:
   1. Try `match_issues(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter)` - returns list of issue dicts
   2. If matches found, append unique IDs to result
   3. Else, check if pattern is literal in plan IDs (queue_order, skipped, cluster.issue_ids)
   4. Else, check if pattern is a cluster name → expand to member IDs
   5. Return deduplicated result list

- `state`: StateModel with `state["issues"]` dict (all scanned issues)
- `patterns`: list of user input strings
- `plan`: PlanModel with clusters dict, queue_order, skipped, overrides
- **Full issues dict available:** `state.get("issues", {})` — can introspect all issue IDs

```python
    _print_pattern_hints()
```

- All issues from detector (`review`)
- All issues in file (`src/foo.py`)
- Issue name — last ::segment of ID (`timing_attack`)
- Glob pattern (`review::*naming*`)
- Cluster name (`my-cluster`)

- **No existing fuzzy matching found** in codebase. The `match_issues()` function at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/resolution.py` (lines 85-96) uses `_matches_pattern()` which is deterministic (no fuzzy/levenshtein distance).
- Only exact matching or glob patterns supported.

**Key Files & Functions:**

1. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py`**
   - **Function:** `_cmd_cluster_list()` (lines 262-276)

   **Current Implementation:**
   ```python
   plan = load_plan()
   clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})  # dict — order is insertion order (Python 3.7+)
   for name, cluster in clusters.items():  # Iterates in insertion order
       member_count = len(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
       # ... prints
   ```
   **IMPORTANT:** Clusters are iterated in **insertion order** (dict key order), NOT in `queue_order`.

2. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema.py`** (lines 160-184)
   - **PlanModel TypedDict:**
     ```python
     class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
         queue_order: list[str]          # Flat list of issue IDs (the priority ordering)
         clusters: dict[str, Cluster]     # Cluster definitions by name
     ```
   - **queue_order** is a flat list of **issue IDs**, NOT cluster IDs
   - **clusters** dict maps cluster name → Cluster object with `issue_ids: list[str]`

**Cluster Order vs Queue Order Relationship:**
- **`queue_order`** is a flat list like: `["issue1", "issue2", "issue3"]`
- Clusters are separate: each cluster has its own `issue_ids` list
- **There is NO cluster-order field in the plan schema**
- Clusters appear in insertion order in the dict

**How to Map Cluster → Earliest Queue Position:**
To find the earliest queue_position for all issues in a cluster:
```python
queue_order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
cluster_issue_ids = set(plan["clusters"]["my-cluster"]["issue_ids"])
earliest_position = None
for idx, issue_id in enumerate(queue_order):
    if issue_id in cluster_issue_ids:
        earliest_position = idx
        break
```

**cluster_reorder Exists:**
Yes! At lines 147-194 in `cluster_handlers.py`:
- **Function:** `_cmd_cluster_reorder(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:`
- **Parameters parsed:**
  - `cluster_names`: comma-separated cluster name(s)
  - `position`: choices = ["top", "bottom", "before", "after", "up", "down"]
  - `target`: optional — target issue/cluster name or integer offset
- **Implementation:** Collects all member IDs from all specified clusters, calls `move_items(plan, all_member_ids, position, target, offset)` to move the entire group as a block

   **Parameters:**
   - `patterns`: list of issue ID patterns (required)
   - `position`: "top" | "bottom" | "before" | "after" | "up" | "down" (required)
   - `target`: optional — issue/cluster ID or integer offset
   
   **Flow:**
   ```python

   2. Resolve patterns to issue_ids via resolve_ids_from_patterns()
   3. Resolve cluster names used as targets to their first/last member ID
   4. Call move_items(plan, issue_ids, position, target, offset)
   5. Log and save
   ```

2. **`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/operations_queue.py`**
   - **Function:** `move_items()` (lines 94-135)
   - **Signature:** `def move_items(plan: PlanModel, issue_ids: list[str], position: str, target: str | None = None, offset: int | None = None) -> int:`
   
   **queue_order Mutation Logic:**
   ```python
   order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   
   # 1. Remove IDs from skipped if present
   for fid in issue_ids:
       skipped.pop(fid, None)
   
   # 2. Remove IDs from current position in order
   for fid in issue_ids:
       if fid in order:
           order.remove(fid)
   
   # 3. Resolve insertion point via _resolve_position()
   idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, issue_ids)
   
   # 4. Insert in original order
   for i, fid in enumerate(issue_ids):
       order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   
   # 5. Track promoted IDs if position == "top"
   if position == "top":
       add_promoted_ids(plan, issue_ids)
   ```

**To Move Items Within a Cluster's Slice:**
Example: Move issue X to be first among all items in cluster Y:
```python
# 1. Get all cluster members in queue order
cluster_name = "my-cluster"
cluster_members = plan["clusters"][cluster_name]["issue_ids"]
queue_order = plan["queue_order"]
ordered_members = [id for id in queue_order if id in set(cluster_members)]

# 2. Find target issue within cluster
target_idx = ordered_members.index("issue-x")

# 3. If not already first, move to before the first cluster member
if target_idx > 0:
    first_cluster_member = ordered_members[0]
    move_items(plan, ["issue-x"], "before", target=first_cluster_member)
```

- **YES, EXISTS** at line 147 in `cluster_handlers.py`

## KEY ARCHITECTURAL INSIGHTS

1. **queue_order is flat:** Issue IDs only, no cluster structure. Clusters are maintained separately.
2. **No cluster-order field:** Clusters appear in dict insertion order only.
3. **Pattern matching:** No fuzzy matching; only exact ID, detector, file path, glob, name-segment, or cluster-name expansion.
4. **Overlap detection:** Implemented at add-time (lines 83-97 in cluster_handlers.py), compares membership >50% threshold.
5. **Dry-run pattern:** Use `action="store_true"`, condition actual mutations, call alternate report functions.
6. **Cluster list is sparse:** Only name, count, auto-tag, description. Queue render collapses auto-clusters into meta-items for richer display.
7. **Cluster reorder exists:** Moves cluster members as a contiguous block via `move_items()`.
8. **Pattern hints are hardcoded:** Shown on every no-match via `_print_pattern_hints()` (lines 29-42).
